THIRD-PARTY LICENCES FOR THE GPUWM-HEX WHEEL'S COMPILED HELPERS
==============================================================================

Written by tools/helper_licences.py; do not edit it by hand.  The tool's own
docstring says why this file exists and how to regenerate or check it.

The programs below, which the wheel carries in hexcore/libexec, are built
from WOOF's Rust workspace (tools/rustwx in the gpuwm repository) and link
third-party crates.  Their grants condition redistribution in binary form on the
notices below travelling with the copy; this file is that.

  [S]  hex_obs_scorecard: the observation scorer (rust/obs-scorecard)
  [B]  rw_mpas_geometry, rw_mpas_stream, rw_mpas_static, rw_mpas_mesh, librw_surface.so, rw_mpas_lbc and the RRFS programs rw_mpas_rrfs_connectivity, rw_mpas_rrfs_damping and rw_mpas_rrfs_deformation: the bundled engine bridges (crate rw-mpas)
  [O]  rw_igra2: the IGRA2 radiosonde door (crate rw-obs)
  [W]  rw_wrfbatch and rw_ensbatch: the renderer and the ensemble renderer (crate rw-wrfbatch)
  [C]  rw_mpas_convert: the chemistry-aware history converter (crate rw-mpas)
  [F]  rw_sfc_series: the surface series producer (crate rw-sfc-series)
  [P]  rw_mpas_physics_pool: the Registry pool reader (rust/physics-pool)
  [R]  rw_s2s: the subseasonal reducer (crate rw-s2s, this tree's tools/rustwx)
  [H]  rw_hex_chem_sources: the chemistry source preparer (rust/chem-sources)
  [E]  rw_s2s_energy: the S2S energy tables' reducer (crate rw-s2s-energy, this tree's tools/rustwx)
  [U]  rw_hex_ugwp_oro: the GSL orographic drag statistics builder (rust/ugwp-oro)

The remaining Rust helpers link the Rust standard library only.
NOTICE section 12 also names the C libraries the helpers link.

WHAT IT COVERS
--------------
  gpuwm commit             aaec6b4be30ddc8f416f44cbc0fecf9244459f5e
  tools/rustwx/Cargo.lock  sha256 124aeed826f08c404df231e049442d5a15656dde6451ea98c63ea9852f9f2c78
  engine-bridges.json      sha256 cc7cb6af84aa9c6c5a95938c89a00f808609e0c0841a4bf227fcd3734bd4694d
  source notice            licenses/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES-bridge-binaries.txt at that commit,
                           sha256 efd582033a1db42135d0a837384ef9c332a785ffcdd781e9109ea59822d33063

The source identity above is the resolved build workspace. The hashed bridge
manifest retains each binary's own source identity and lock override;
its top-level identity is the fallback for entries without an override.
  scorer resolved against  gpuwm commit 82e16d580ae436003d3598a8127baef8b7498519,
                           Cargo.lock sha256 4c0c6e43d75c94347d1225b91b148c385f9741de4302c067510295779e91ec87
  [B] also resolved against gpuwm commit 5599a17b919c12cdaf98ebad8d0b08c8e8532e78,
                           Cargo.lock sha256 4c0c6e43d75c94347d1225b91b148c385f9741de4302c067510295779e91ec87
  [W][C] resolved against   gpuwm commit 5599a17b919c12cdaf98ebad8d0b08c8e8532e78,
                           Cargo.lock sha256 4c0c6e43d75c94347d1225b91b148c385f9741de4302c067510295779e91ec87

The crate lists use cargo tree -e normal,no-proc-macro, with all targets
for the engine workspace and x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu for the separate
surface-series and pool-reader projects. These are conservative package
closures; linker removal can leave fewer crates in a binary. Numbered
texts come from the engine notice. SOURCE references come from the exact
resolved crate source when that notice does not inventory its version.
A [RADAR-DEPS-n] reference is a text the source notice carries in its WOOF
simulated radar appendix, which is where it inventories that crate.

AND BSD-3-Clause. The Registry pool reader is MIT. rw-s2s, rw-s2s-energy and
rust/chem-sources (hex-chem-sources) and rust/ugwp-oro (hex-ugwp-oro) are this project's own code (Apache-2.0).

==============================================================================
2.  NOTICES THAT ARE NOT CRATE LICENCES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Some of WOOF's own crates above port third-party code into themselves.
The source notice's paragraphs for the ones these programs link follow
unchanged; where they say "this bundle" they mean WOOF's bridge bundle,
and here they cover the programs in this wheel that link the crate named.

2.3  MPAS -- Los Alamos National Security, LLC and UCAR  (the rw_mpas_* binaries)

The five rw_mpas_* executables in this bundle -- mesh, static, init, convert,
lbc -- are built from tools/rustwx/crates/rw-mpas, a Rust port of parts of
MPAS-Atmosphere v8.4.1: init_atm_case_lbc, the vertical and horizontal
interpolation of mpas_init_atm_vinterp.F and mpas_init_atm_hinterp.F, the
gravity-wave-drag fields of mpas_init_atm_gwd.F, the deformation and advection
weights of mpas_atm_advection.F and core_sw/mpas_sw_advection.F, mpas_in_cell
from mpas_geometry_utils.F, the constants of mpas_constants.F, and the soil
and surface companions of mpas_atmphys_initialize_real.F.  static_fields, also
in this bundle, is linked by those binaries.

MPAS's licence requires this text to ship with the binary form, and its
government-rights paragraph requires a modified derivative to be clearly
marked: this port is such a derivative and is named rw_mpas, not MPAS.

Copyright (c) 2013-2019,  Los Alamos National Security, LLC (LANS) (Ocean: LA-CC-13-047;
Land Ice: LA-CC-13-117) and the University Corporation for Atmospheric Research (UCAR).

All rights reserved.

2.4  NumPy -- NumPy Developers  (the float32 kernels inside static_fields)

The static_fields library in this bundle contains
tools/rustwx/crates/static-fields/src/projection/npmath.rs, which ports four
of NumPy's own float32 SIMD kernels -- sin, cos, exp and log -- from
numpy/_core/src/umath/loops_trigonometric.dispatch.cpp and
numpy/_core/src/umath/loops_exponent_log.dispatch.c.src at v2.2.6, with
coefficients from npy_simd_data.h.  Same constants, same fused-multiply-add
sequence: the port's contract is byte parity with NumPy.  The rw_mpas_*
executables link that library, so this notice covers them too.

The text below is numpy/LICENSE.txt at v2.2.6, the vintage ported.  Current
upstream reads "Copyright (c) 2005-2025, NumPy Developers." -- a year roll and
nothing else.

Copyright (c) 2005-2024, NumPy Developers.
All rights reserved.

Redistribution and use in source and binary forms, with or without
modification, are permitted provided that the following conditions are
met:

    * Redistributions of source code must retain the above copyright
       notice, this list of conditions and the following disclaimer.

    * Redistributions in binary form must reproduce the above
       copyright notice, this list of conditions and the following
       disclaimer in the documentation and/or other materials provided
       with the distribution.

    * Neither the name of the NumPy Developers nor the names of any
       contributors may be used to endorse or promote products derived
       from this software without specific prior written permission.

THIS SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED BY THE COPYRIGHT HOLDERS AND CONTRIBUTORS
"AS IS" AND ANY EXPRESS OR IMPLIED WARRANTIES, INCLUDING, BUT NOT
LIMITED TO, THE IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS FOR
A PARTICULAR PURPOSE ARE DISCLAIMED. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE COPYRIGHT

2.5  Arm optimized-routines and CORE-MATH  (the libm inside static_fields)

The static_fields library in this bundle contains the first-party crate
tools/rustwx/crates/rw-libm, which the WPS orographic projection calls
instead of the platform C library so that its results are glibc 2.43's on
every platform.  It transcribes Arm's optimized-routines sinf, cosf,
expf, logf and powf (math/sinf.c, math/cosf.c, math/sincosf.h,
math/sincosf_data.c, math/expf.c, math/exp2f_data.c, math/logf.c,
math/logf_data.c, math/powf.c, math/powf_log2_data.c) and CORE-MATH's
correctly rounded tanf, atanf, atan2f, log10f, asin and acos (revision
284b3b0e198042c38f5c30316f696786b10816b0).  The rw_mpas_* executables and
rw_mlexport link that library, so this notice covers them too.  Both are
MIT; the texts follow.

The CPU preprocessing bridge also compiles the pinned FP32 powf and tables
in tools/grib1_bridge/src/glibc239_math.rs, transcribed from the same
Arm optimized-routines implementation retained in woof/core/noahmp_libm.py.
The 2017-2018 Arm copyright and MIT grant below apply to that compiled
copy. Its separate FP64 host-math entries call the platform C library and
do not transcribe its implementation.

Arm optimized-routines:
